## Rules of Conduct for Public Comment at Council Meetings $ ^{*} $

Members of the public may speak at a Council meeting upon recognition by the Mayor. Public comment may only occur during periods designated on the agenda for public comment or a public hearing. A person may speak for up to two (2) minutes during each of the two public comment periods on agenda items or non-agenda items. Waivers of the time requirement may only be granted in the discretion of the Mayor, and waivers to speak at a time other than a designated comment period may only be granted by the Council. Any person wishing to make a presentation longer than two minutes or requiring audio-visual equipment is asked to contact the City Clerk requesting to appear on a future agenda.

A person may only address Council from the podium. Only one person may occupy the podium at a time. All remarks are to be directed to the Mayor and Council. Speakers are not to engage in direct dialog with other meeting attendees.

Any person who violates the Rules of Conduct, disturbs the peace at the meeting, and/or interferes with the meeting may be warned, ordered to be seated, removed, and/or ticketed.

 $ ^{*} $This summarizes Council Resolution 04-18. Complete Rules, including guidelines for considering waivers, are available in the Council Chambers and from the City Clerk.

City of South Lyon

Regular City Council Meeting

February 26, 2024

Mayor Kennedy opened the meeting at 7:30 p.m.

ROLL CALL

Roll Call: Mayor Kennedy and Councilmembers: Bogert, Dilg, Hansen, Kivell, Mosier and Kurtzweil, Also present: City Manager Zelenak, Fire Chief Thorington, Police Chief Baaki, Benefit and Finance Administrator Tiernan and Deputy Clerk/Treasurer Pieper.

## PUBLIC COMMENT

Ryan Cottingham of 601 S Latayite stated he is here to talk about the last meeting. He then thanked Councilmembers Kurtzweil, Dig and Bogert and he appreciates that they stuck to their word and convictions. He then stated Mayor Kennedy, Councilmember Kivell, Hansen and Mosier he wants to express his disappointment. He stated he couldn't believe watching that video and it was hard to watch that hour long session. It was chaotic, all over the place. He further stated the meeting previous to that, everyone agreed to and voted to send that developer with the direction to come back with a new plan, which they did. And then for some reason you are making motions on the first plan and disregarding the word and convictions that you set forth to protect the natural, pristine, untouched wetland. There are so many suggestions for the traffic fixes. He stated Council made it clear that traffic is more important than our natural resources, not safety, but traffic. He again stated there were many fixes offered during the

<a id="p4"></a>
*[Page 4 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=4)* (read by OCR; check the original)

conversation that would rectify the problem, which is a man-made problem from us and vehicles. He said what we can't fix is when we rip through that wetland and we bisect it. When we touch this untouched thing that has been there before cars, we can't go back and fix that. He then said he is sure the environmental report that the developer is going to pay for will say, oh sure it will be fine, but it won't be like it was before. It won't be pristine and untouched. He then said for the 4 of you, he expresses his deepest disappointment. Kurtzweil, Dilg and Bogert, thank you for standing your ground.

Erin Cottingim of 601 S Lafayette stated she is going to say a lot of what he just said. To Kurtzweil, Dilg and Bogert, thank you for staying true to your word and continuing to vote to protect our wetlands. She stated she appreciates them and their integrity. Councilmembers Kivell, Hansen, Mosier and Mayor Kennedy, she stated she wants to express her disappointment in their going back on their decision to protect the wetlands. She said they had a chance to protect 3,000 square feet of wetlands, and they voted not to, because 3,000 cars drive by there every day. She then said the developer was willing to change their plan and save the wetlands, and they voted not to protect the wetlands. You voted to line up driveways, no matter what the cost and that shouldn't take precedent over the mental health of our community. She then stated everyone voted unanimously to protect the wetlands, and there was an alternate plan to save the wetlands, and she thought they all wanted to protect the wetlands because you said you did. The developer said he would happily change the plan and leave the wetlands alone. He wasn't threatening to back out, but he drew a new plan around the wetlands. He seemed eager to please and open to revisions, and she understands the delay in projects, all too well. She stated she sympathizes. She stated you asked him to make a second plan, then didn't bother to hear it. She then said if the existing entrance was so dangerous, why was it originally approved. Its because a traffic study that was done at the time so it should be approved, and since then there has been one accident. She stated its not nothing, but we can all agree it's not a big number. She said she doesn't hear anyone talk about crosswalks or lowering the speed limit, only that traffic flow is the important issue. Not safety. She stated there was so many solutions mentioned, such as a directional island, suggested by a traffic study. She said they should not get a traffic study for the new plan considered. They know the environmental company the developer hires is going to say they recommend it to move forward. She further stated they will get their half page that is what they are hired to do. She said they all know by dissecting a pristine irreplaceable wetland is dangerous. She said they all know this will make an impact on our community. All they had to do is approve their second plan. If time is such an issue, Council should have approved the new plans so they could move forward. You have an opportunity here and you don't have to choose safety and environmental concerns, you can have the best of both worlds and that is the second plan. It is frustrating.

Mariel Cottingham 11964 Greenway Circle stated she is 14 years old and she is embarrassed of the decision to get rid of the wetlands. She stated we have gotten rid of many wetlands already and you're already seeing some consequences. She plays for the South Lyon Football team and they train on Griswold Road, and the parking lot was flooded. The reason was because the wetlands was there. And there is a huge pothole where the flooding was. She said she would hate to grow up and show her children her hometown, and say oh, they destroyed the beautiful wetlands for a road that disrupted the wetlands.

##  $ \underline{\text{DISCUSSION}} $- Downtown

DDA Director Mack stated they had another Winter Farmers Market at the VFW over the weekend and it was pretty good. He said they had 28 vendors even though a couple cancelled. He then stated the outdoor market season is looking good and they have some old and new vendor applications coming in.

<a id="p5"></a>
*[Page 5 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=5)* (read by OCR; check the original)

He then stated March 2 $ ^{nd} $ will be the 3 $ ^{rd} $ Carnivale event from 4-7 and we have 2-character artists, a tarot card reader, street performers and food trucks and the build a bear truck will be there. They will be passing out beads and masks and sidewalk chalk. The Learning Experience is the sponsor of the event, they will also have face painting. He then stated Main Street Oakland County facilitated a joint discussion with South Lyon, Lyon Township, Wixom and Highland. He stated they were discussing certain ways our communities can collaborate and they are calling it South by Southwest Group. It's just to exchange ideas and build a network. He further stated this week Oakland County Main Street will be in town to sign businesses up for the online shopping platform. They have 4 businesses on the schedule right now. Councilmember Hansen asked about the electric vehicle chargers. Mr. Mack stated they are still working with DTE for the permits and the grant funding is in place. They indicated they are still looking at the first quarter of the year. Councilmember Kivell asked if the group meeting is for brainstorming ideas. Mr. Mack stated its for exchanging ideas, and to share some events or activities.

## FIRE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Thorington stated they are currently at 204 instances. There was a structural fire in town yesterday and we had two on shift, and two on site having coffee, so we had a truck leave with 4 firefighters and it was a quick knockdown. It was extending outside of the house up into the eves and they knocked it down quick and saved the house. Our cadet Soave recently turned 18 and has his Fire 1 & 2 through the Howell Fire Academy and he's currently in the EMT class and should be completed by June and then he'll be a full-fledged Firefighter. He then pointed out some highlights of the Fire Departments annual report. He stated Micah DeMeyer was the 2023 Firefighter of the year. 2023 was the busiest year for the Fire Department history. He then stated between 2018 and 2023 was the most significant increase. 2020 was an anomaly due to covid. All the departments around South Lyon went down in 2020. There are multiple theories on why the rise across the state and the U.S. Everything from increase in population, increased elderly population, more people coming through the community, and a different mindset on when to call 911, and its hard-to-get doctors' appointments, so if people are feeling sick, they call 911. It can be construed by some as abusing the system, or not the best of the use for it, but he feels if anyone has a need to call them, they are like problem solves and they will show up and help them or at least steer them in the right direction. He would choose to have them call and enson the side of safety. He further stated their EMS calls were 64% and the balance was fire rescue and other service calls, which is pretty standard across the industry. We had 47 mutual aids given and so received. He stated it looks like an off number between the given and the received, but we give aid to multiple communities and only receive from one. He further stated they feel the mutual aid is important because they have automatic aid with Green Oak Township, on both sides of us so anytime we're dispatched to a structure fire they automatically start moving in our direction, but we can cancel at any time. It is really the only way for us to get the minimum recommended NFPA number on scene which is 16, without that we would have a hard time getting to that number and its great training for our people who can't get the hands on experience of being in fires, which we can replicate the best we can on our training facility with fires, but it's not the same as live structure fires with the chaos that that ensues around you in an uncontrolled environment. Councilmember Bogert stated he wants to acknowledge Chief Thorington and his staff for a well put together annual report.

## POLICE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Baaki stated he wanted to update everyone on FEMA. He said he has been working with them to set up a recovery center, for the damage from last August's storms. We were able to confirm they will be using the conference rooms at city hall because it meets their needs for the facility. They will be setting up Sunday March 10th, and they will be here from March 11th thru March 16th from 8:00am to 6:30pm.

They will update the information on their website and Facebook page. He further stated they have been walking around subdivisions passing out flyers. He then reminded the residents they will be going around the area and they will have vests on and credentials. They'll be asking if you need assistance, and if so, they will give you the flyer with the information, or residents can meet with FEMA at the recovery center at city hall on those dates. He then stated the Citizens Police Academy starts on March 6th, and there are already a few people enrolled. There are applications available at the police station, and on their website and Facebook page. If anyone is interested it is a great experience.

UNFINISHED BUSINESS- None

## NEW BUSINESS

1. Purchase of new Boerger PL300 rotary lobe pump

Water/Sewer Department Superintendent Beason stated they are requesting the purchase of a new Boerger PL 300 rotary lobe pump to replace a failing piston-type sludge transfer pump. He stated the new rotary lobe design is easier to maintain and allows for better performance and efficiency. Recently the wastewater department has replaced 3 older piston pumps with the Boerger lobe type and has found these pumps to be very reliable with less maintenance. He further stated this is a scheduled purchase for this year's budget and can be purchased out of capital improvements line item 592-557-972 in the amount of $14,982.00 Councilmember Kurtzweil asked why this isn't going out for bid. Superintendent Beason stated this is the only company that will provide this. They are the representative for Michigan for that pump. He stated it is the type of lobe that we need for our facility.

## MANAGER'S REPORT

City Manager Zelenak stated we will have the Washington Manor Development and the West Lake Development on future agendas. We are moving forward with the closing of the sale of 318 W Lake, and he will update Council with the closing date. He then stated Chief Baaki updated everyone on FEMA and he wants to let the residents know they will be here between March 10th and March 17th and they will get more information out when we receive more information and data. He stated he will be scheduling the budget meeting in March when he has dates that all Councilmembers can attend. He then stated we have had early voting available since Saturday the 17th and we've had approximately 120 voters come and vote at City Hall. He stated that doesn't include the absentee ballots that were issued and returned. The Clerk's office has been working diligently getting ready for the early voting and the election, as a matter of fact, the City Clerk is in the office working with the state on some items related to tomorrow's election. He then reminded everyone to thank the election workers and they have been working hard to get everything ready for tomorrow's election to run properly. He then reminded everyone of Carnivale this Saturday and it's supposed to be 58 degrees. Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she has been getting many questions for an update on the Andover Creek bridge. City Manager Zelenak stated the Engineer updated him last week that the bridge will be completed soon, then it will be painted and installation. Then the concrete will be added. He stated we are hoping for April.

## COUNCIL COMMENTS

Councilmember Bogert stated in the recent past we have had meetings that extended past 9:00 and 10:00. He then stated he realizes that makes for a long day for our employees that come in early that morning. He further stated he did a survey for 11 municipalities, and most start at 7:00 p.m. He would like Council to discuss changing the time for our Council meetings to change to 7:00 p.m.

Councilmember Hansen stated he wants to remind everyone of the 20th annual creek clean up on April 21 $ ^{st} $ from 9:00 am to 1:00 p.m. and the volunteers will get a t-shirt because it is a milestone event. He stated they are hoping to have many guests involved, and volunteer record for this event.

Councilmember Mosier stated we are going to have great weekend weather and she's looking forward to seeing everyone out there. She then wished the Clerk's office staff a lot of success tomorrow and get through the day. She then stated in March there will be 3 indoor Farmers Market and she hopes to see everyone there.

Councilmember Dilg stated she was here to get her training for the Election, and she looked in the eyes of the Clerk's office and they want the elections to go well, and if anyone thinks there are any flaws in our elections, please become a poll worker and see how things work. She further stated she hopes everyone will be patient and kind to the poll workers.

Councilmember Kivell stated he attended the pancake breakfast for the barn and there were a lot of people there and it was very nice and we had a good visit with our Representative Jason Morgan and he is a nice guy and a lot of understanding of what the object of his vision is. He seems to be very friendly and he will be at the creek clean up as well.

She stated the staff is here if you have any questions, so please treat the staff professionally.

Mayor Kennedy stated he wanted to remind everyone about the South Lyon Area Creek Cleanup that will be held on Sunday, April 21 $ ^{st} $ from 9am until 1pm. Gather the family and come to the Michigan Seamless Tube parking lot at 9am to get an assigned area. From there folks will spread out through the community to help clear debris from the waterways and collect trash from the roadways and parks. He further stated lunch will be provided at 12 Noon by Taormina's Pizza, so everyone should make plans now to come out and Show Your Love for Lyon.

# AGENDA NOTE Consent Agenda: Item #1

MEETING DATE: March 11, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: City Manager

AGENDA TOPIC: Resignation of Mack Dobbie from Cultural Arts Commission

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: We have received a letter from Mack Dobbie resigning from the Cultural Arts Commission.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Email from Mack Dobbie resigning from Cultural Arts Commission

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Accept the resignation of Mack Dobbie from the Cultural Arts Commission

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by ___ supported by ___ to accept the resignation of Mack Dobbie from Cultural Arts Commission

<a id="p27"></a>
*[Page 27 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=27)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Over the past couple years I have enjoyed our time serving the community as ambassadors of art and creativity. I am proud of the accomplishments we've made, and the attention it has received. The boards throughout town, and the shows we've hosted, have given the artists in the area a voice, and a place to share their passions.

I have personally been wrestling with being able to balance fully investing in my role as a commissioner, or working to achieve some of the personal goals I have been putting on hold. It has been a tough choice, but I have decided that I am going to lay down my role on the committee in pursuit of other achievements and career advances.

I would like to thank each of you for the opportunities presented to serve as a commissioner, and for the support over the past couple years. My last council meeting will be February of 2024. If there are any items needed to be handed off, please let me know and we can make arrangements to do so.

Sincerely,

Sincerely,

Mack Dobbie

<a id="p28"></a>
*[Page 28 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=28)* (read by OCR; check the original)

# AGENDA NOTE Unfinished Business Item #1

MEETING DATE: March 11, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Recommendation from Planning Commission on November 16, 2023

AGENDA TOPIC: Second Reading of Ordinance to Amend the Official Zoning Map of the City of South Lyon by conditionally rezoning portions of the total 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel #21-30-126-003, zoned I-1, Light Industrial, as follows: 24.39+/- acres of RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential and 1.63+/- acres R-3, Single-Family Residential. The remaining 8.14+/- acres will remain as I-1, Light Industrial.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: Elevate Land Holdings, LLC has petitioned the City and requested a Conditional Rezoning of a portion of a 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel #21-30-126-003, to allow 252 new multiple-family residential units, 3 single-family residential lots, and the remaining portion of the parcel to be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development. The petitioner's request is pursuant to MCL 125.3405, which authorizes rezoning with conditions voluntarily offered by the owner of the property. The Planning Commission held a public hearing for the request on November 16, 2023 and recommended approval of the request for the conditional rezoning based on the conditions voluntarily offered in writing by the owner.

The ordinance and preliminary site plan originally were before City Council at the January 22, 2024 meeting, where after discussion regarding the proposed re-location of the entrance drive off of Ten Mile and the potential impacts to the wetlands, the Council tabled the item to allow the applicant to revise the offered conditions and provide an updated site plan. The request was back before the Council at the February 8, 2024 meeting with the updated drive location. After discussion about the alternative plans, the City Council requested environmental impact information of the re-located drive. The City Council approved the first reading of the Ordinance (associated with the site plan dated November 8, 2023) and postponed action on the site plan (dated November 8, 2023). The applicant has provided a wetland impact study for consideration by the City Council, along with the wetland delineation study that was conducted at the time of the site plans.

The materials provided are related to the ordinance amendment for which the Council approved the first reading. Please note that next steps involve  $ \underline{\text{three motions}} $ – second reading of the ordinance, approval of the conditional rezoning agreement and authorization for the agreement to be signed, and the preliminary site plan.

## MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS:

Please note that materials included in this packet are only  $ \underline{\text{new}} $ materials since the last meeting. These are listed below. NOTE: The market analysis, traffic study, and application are included in this packet as an exhibit of the ordinance draft, but the content of these materials has not changed from the previous submittal.

- Draft ordinance to amend the official zoning map of the City of South Lyon conditionally rezoning portions of parcel # 21-30-126-003 from I-1 Light Industrial, including 24.39+/- acres of RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential and 1.63+/- acres R-3, Single-Family Residential. The proposed ordinance contains the proposed Conditional Rezoning Agreement verbatim, but the Agreement will be presented separately for approval (Application, market analysis, and traffic study included only as exhibit, have not changed)

- Conditional Rezoning Agreement – to be approved as a separate item (application, market analysis, traffic study, and site plan included as exhibits)

- Wetland Impact Statement dated 2/22/2024

- Wetland Delineation Report dated 7/7/2023 (as referenced in Wetland Impact Statement)

For materials previously reviewed by the Council that are applicable but not new/updated at this time, please see January 22, 2024 Council packet. This includes the following:

• Preliminary site plan dated 11/8/2023

CIB Planning Review dated 11/15/2023

- HRC Review dated 7/25/2024

- Fire Department Review dated 8/15/2023

WHEREAS, the Property is currently zoned I-1 (Light Industrial) and Owner desires to develop a mixed-use development to include a Multiple Family Residential and a Single Family Residential development project on a portion of the Property which is not permitted under the Property's I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district, and to develop a portion of the Property in conformance with the Property's existing I-1 Light Industrial zoning classification;

WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, 2008 PA 110, MCL 125.3405 et seq., as amended, certain conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner of the Property, may become a condition of rezoning the Property;

WHEREAS, on or about June 6, 2023, a representative of the Owner, submitted a Conditional Rezoning Application for the Property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings, and a preliminary landscape plan, voluntarily offering, in writing, certain conditions to rezone the Property to PUD (Planned Unit Development);

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission held a public hearing, after publication of notice thereof, on the Owner's Conditional Rezoning Application;

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended approval of the Owner's request for conditional rezoning based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by Owner in writing;

WHEREAS, after receiving a favorable recommendation from the Planning Commission, but before the recommendation was presented to City Council, the Owner, City Planner, City Attorney and City Manager reevaluated the Owner's request to conditionally rezone to PUD (Planned Unit Development) and agreed that the conditional rezoning should have been to RM-3 (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 (Single Family Residential) for a portion of the Property.

COMMENCING AT THE SOUTHWEST CORNER OF SECTION 19, ALSO BEING THE NORTHWEST CORNER OF SECTION 30, T1N-R7E, LYON TOWNSHIP OAKLAND COUNTY, MICHIGAN; THENCE S03°14′44″E 675.28 FEET ALONG THE WEST LINE OF SAID SECTION 30 TO THE POINT OF BEGINNING; THENCE N86°45′16″E 99.38 FEET; THENCE 119.52 FEET ALONG A 260.50 FOOT RADIUS CURVE TO THE RIGHT, CHORD BEARING S80°29′03″E 118.47 FEET; THENCE 91.71 FEET ALONG A 248.60 FOOT RADIUS CURVE TO THE LEFT, CHORD BEARING S80°31′48″E 91.19 FEET; THENCE N86°18′23″E 546.95 FEET; THENCE N73°54′06″E 62.23 FEET; THENCE N82°16′29″E 26.17 FEET; THENCE N86°23′35″E 20.85 FEET; THENCE N58°38′36″E 107.81 FEET; THENCE N60°35′1′O"E 42.96 FEET; THENCE S55°40′56″E 320.66 FEET; THENCE S46°19′32″E 181.71 FEET TO NORTH-SOUTH 1/4 LINE OF SECTION 30; THENCE S86°19′40″W 1470.41 FEET TO THE WEST LINE OF SECTION 30; THENCE CONTINUING ALONG SAID WEST LINE N03°14′44″W 295.36 FEET TO THE POINT OF BEGINNING, CONTAINING 8.14 ACRES OF LAND, MORE OR LESS, AND SUBJECT EASEMENTS AND RESTRICTIONS OF RECORD, IF ANY.

<a id="p36"></a>
*[Page 36 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=36)* (read by OCR; check the original)

WHEREAS, on _____ 2024, the City Council approved the request for Conditional Rezoning and adopted Ordinance No. _____ based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner in writing which are set forth in this Agreement and the attached Exhibit A; and

NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and the mutual promises hereinafter set forth, the City and Owner agree as follows:

1.  $ \underline{\text{Conditional Rezoning}} $. Pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, MCL 125.3405, Owner agrees to limit the development and use of the Property as set forth in the rezoning conditions below as a condition of rezoning. Upon execution of this Agreement by the parties, the City shall effectuate the conditional rezoning of the Residential Property from I-1 District (Limited Industrial) to the RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), as legally described herein, on the City's official Zoning Map. This Agreement is valid and entered into on a voluntary basis and represents a permissible exercise of authority by the City pursuant to MCL 125.3405.

2.  $ \underline{\text{Rezoning Conditions}} $. Owner agrees to develop and use the Property in accordance with the following conditions which have been offered voluntarily in writing by the Owner as conditions to the rezoning:

a. Development and use of the Property shall be for a phased development of Multiple Family Residential and Single Family Residential on the Residential Property, and a phased development of Industrial buildings on the remaining portion of the Property zoned I-1 District (Light Industrial) in substantial conformance with the preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and in accordance with the Time Limits described in Paragraph 6, below, consisting of:

(1) 24.40\pm/ - acres of Multiple Family Residential Property, 1.63\pm/ - acres of Single-Family Residential Property and 8.14\pm/ - acres of Light Industrial Property;

(2) 252 Multiple Family 1-, 2-, and 3-bedroom residential units on the Property rezoned RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential);

(3) 3 single family residential detached dwelling units on the Property rezoned R-3 District (Single Family Residential); and

<a id="p37"></a>
*[Page 37 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=37)* (read by OCR; check the original)

(4) Approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space on the I-1 District (Light Industrial).

(collectively referred to as the "Project"). The Project will be developed in the phases as depicted on the preliminary site plan attached as Exhibit B and as follows: Phase 1 of the Industrial development, shall include one 19,840 +/- sq. ft. industrial building; Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 4-7, 84 units total; Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 8-12, 84 units total, and the clubhouse with ancillary amenities; Phase 3 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 13-18, 84 units total. The future development of the remaining I-1 (Light Industrial) zoned property and the R-3 (Single Family Residential) zoned property will be developed in accordance with the underlying zoning district.

b. Development of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development to occur concurrently.

c. Constructed as part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development, the paving of 1,088 lineal feet of Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property.

d. At the time of Dixboro Road paving, the pedestrian pathway ultimately connecting to Huron Valley Trail, measuring 1,088 lineal feet along Dixboro Rd, will be installed from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property line;

e. As part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential Development, a payment of $36,200 to the City of South Lyon will be made for the future construction of the remaining 724 lineal feet from the southerly Property line and Huron Valley Trail.

f. As a part of Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development, a pedestrian pathway (connecting to the pedestrian pathway constructed in Phase 1) will be constructed along Lake Street from the corner of Lake Street and Dixboro Road to South Hagadorn Road connecting to the existing sidewalk that leads to downtown.

g. Provided that all necessary permits are secured by the applicable state, county, city or other governmental agency, the existing Lake Street driveway approach serving Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) will be removed and relocated during Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development in order to improve public and traffic safety. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

h. Owner shall secure and record all necessary shared access, use, utility, facilities, parking and other easements between the Project and adjacent development Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) as required by and in a form approved by the City.

i. Minimum floor area reduction to allow for 1-bedroom units to be a minimum of 600 sq. ft., 2-bedroom units to be a minimum of 768 sq. ft. and 3-bedroom units to be

<a id="p38"></a>
*[Page 38 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=38)* (read by OCR; check the original)

a minimum of 936 sq. ft. to meet market demand for 1 - 3 bedroom units and to allow preservation of nine (9) acres of wetlands and 50% of the existing woodlands.

572 parking spaces (which includes parking spaces within the existing Westlake Place Apartments, (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005), which will be available for use within the Project) provided for use by the Multiple Family Residential Property as recommended in the traffic study conducted by Fleis and Vandenbrink, which reduction will reduce impervious surfaces, increase available open space and area for community garden, recreation courts and a dog park.

k. 223 parking spaces provided for the industrial use to be located on the portion of the Property within the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district. 67 parking spaces shall be provided for the Phase 1 Industrial development and the additional parking spaces are proposed because tenants and/or users have not been identified or secured for the Industrial use. Once a site plan is submitted for the specific configuration of the remaining Light Industrial district, the City shall determine the required number of parking spaces required or shall cause the Owner to conduct a parking and/or traffic study to establish a basis for the City to consider the appropriate number of parking spaces required. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

For the efficient use of the Property, considering the Project meets several master plan goals, the buffering requirement of the City's Zoning Ordinance between industrial and residential uses must be waived and, once tenants are secured and a site plan submitted, the City shall determine the appropriate buffer requirement considering the proposed industrial use.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Use of the Property}} $. Development and use of the Property shall be in substantial conformance with the approved preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit B, and shall be subject to and in conformance with the approval of the final site plan for the Property by the City Planning Commission. The parties agree that minor revisions to the approved final site plan which, in the sole discretion of the City Manager, do not significantly modify the proposed development or use of the Property or the site plan, may be considered and approved by the City administratively without the necessity of review and approval by the Planning Commission and/or without amendment of this Agreement and exhibits hereto.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Laws}} $. Except as modified herein, development and use of the Property shall comply with all other applicable state, county and local requirements for land

<a id="p39"></a>
*[Page 39 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=39)* (read by OCR; check the original)

development and use, ordinances, codes, standards, regulations, requirements, and engineering design standards and requirements.

5.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Conditions}} $. Once commenced and established, the development and use of the Property must remain in compliance with the conditions of the conditional rezoning Final Site Plan and as provided in this Agreement. Any failure to comply with a condition contained herein shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance and shall be punishable as provided for therein. Additionally, any such violation shall be deemed a nuisance per se and subject to abatement as provided for by law.

6.  $ \underline{\text{Time Limits}} $. The City and Owner agree that the following approvals and activities are conditions to this Agreement and if not completed within the following time limits, the Residential Property may revert back to the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning classification:

a. Final site plan approval within twelve (12) months from the date of this Agreement;

b. A building permit for the Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential and the Phase 1 Industrial development thereof, if applicable, is obtained within one (1) year from the date of final site plan approval;

c. A building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential.

d. A building permit for Phase 3 of the Multiple-Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential.

e. A final certificate of occupancy for the entire Multiple Family Residential, or Phase 2, or Phase 3 thereof if applicable, is obtained within thirty (30) months from the date the building permit was issued for said Phase.

7.  $ \underline{\text{Extension of Time Limits}} $. The City, in the City's exclusive discretion, may agree to extend the time limits in Paragraph 6 upon receipt of a written request from the Owner but the City shall not be required to grant such request for extension. Any extension granted shall be in writing signed by both parties.

<a id="p40"></a>
*[Page 40 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=40)* (read by OCR; check the original)

8.  $ \underline{\text{City Right to Rezone}} $. In the event that the time limit conditions in Paragraph 6 are not met, nor extended upon mutual agreement of the parties pursuant to Paragraph 7; nothing in this Agreement shall be deemed to prohibit the City from rezoning all or any portion of the Property which is subject to this Agreement to another zoning district. Any rezoning shall be conducted in compliance with the City's Zoning Ordinance and the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act.

9. Owner Acknowledgments. Owner acknowledges that it voluntarily offered the conditions contained in this Agreement, including all Exhibits, in connection with the request for conditional rezoning of the Property and that such conditions were offered in writing. Owner acknowledges that upon the approval of the requested conditional rezoning and this Agreement becoming effective, the development and use of the Property shall conform thereafter with the terms and conditions of the conditional rezoning, this Agreement, and all the requirements regulating development and use within the zoning district as modified by the voluntarily offered conditions and with this Agreement and that any failure to fully comply with such conditions and/or this Agreement shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance, and shall be punishable accordingly. Owner acknowledges and agrees that the terms and conditions of this Agreement are necessary and roughly proportional to the burden imposed by the conditional rezoning of the Property, and are therefore necessary to ensure that public services and facilities will be capable of accommodating the development and use and the increased service of facility loads caused by the development and use, to protect the natural environment and conserve natural resources, to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses of land, and to promote the use of the Property in a socially and economically feasible manner, and to achieve other legitimate objectives authorized by law. Owner agrees that the terms and conditions herein do not constitute a taking of property for any purpose or a violation of any

<a id="p41"></a>
*[Page 41 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=41)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Constitutional right, and Owner agrees to be bound by each and every provision and conditions of this Agreement. Owner acknowledges and agrees that if the conditional rezoning becomes void for any reason, then no further development shall take place and no permits shall be issued unless and until a new zoning district classification for the Property has been established.

10.  $ \underline{\text{Entire Agreement}} $. This Agreement together with any agreements referenced herein, constitutes the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the subject of this Agreement.

11.  $ \underline{\text{Binding Effect, Running with the Land}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on and inure to the benefit of the parties and their respective successors, heirs, assigns and transferees, and shall run with the land.

12.  $ \underline{\text{Amendments}} $. The terms of this Agreement may be amended, changed, or modified, but only by written agreement executed by the required parties. Any amendment of this Agreement shall be recorded in the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

13.  $ \underline{\text{Governing Law}} $. This Agreement shall be governed by, construed, and enforced in accordance with Michigan law.

14.  $ \underline{\text{Authority to Execute}} $. The parties each represent and state that the individuals signing this Agreement are fully authorized to execute this document and bind their respective parties to the terms and conditions contained herein.

15.  $ \underline{\text{Enforcement}} $. In the event of a proceeding to enforce this Agreement, a prevailing party may seek to recover its costs and attorney fees in addition to any other applicable and available relief.

16.  $ \underline{\text{Joint Drafting}} $. No provision of this Agreement shall be construed against or interpreted to the disadvantage of one party against any other party by any court or other

City of South Lyon

Planning Commission and Zoning Board of Appeals

335 S. Warren Street

South Lyon, MI 48178

RE: Westlake

To Whom It May Concern,

We are very excited to submit the attached applications and plans for the project we are calling Westlake. Please accept this supplement to our Site Plan Review and Rezoning Application.

This application includes the current project known as Westlake Place Apartments, and the primary addition of the Industrially zoned property to the South and East, commonly known as the ‘Chuck Smith’ property or ‘West End Industrial’. The existing Industrial property is vacant and has been vacant for quite some time. We believe this vacancy is due to several reasons, which we are attempting to address and resolve in the present application. At the same time, we are proposing an important addition to the housing supply within the City, in a market segment that is substantially underserved. Our application proposes a combination of uses that we feel balances these needs, provides for a healthy transition between them, and provides the City with important public amenities that benefit the entire community.

Below are supplemental comments to the site plan review and rezoning applications for your reference and in an effort explain our application more completely.

Why, in our opinion, the requested rezoning is necessary for the presentation and enjoyment of substantial property rights, and why such change will not be detrimental to the public welfare, nor the property of other persons located in the vicinity:

We are proposing to contribute to the housing needs within the City and satisfy the industrial needs within the City with the addition of 3 single family residence parcels, 255 multifamily units and the retention of an appropriate level of industrially zoned land. We believe that this proposal reflects a balance of industrial and multi-family residential that prudently allocates each according to the outstanding demand for both. The proposed industrial provides a perfect transition to the adjacent, existing heavy industrial (Michigan Seamless Tube). The multi-family component borders the industrial, with an enhanced transition including garages / storage areas and a substantially landscaped perimeter further separating the residential units from the industrial property.

To the north, the property is bordered by residential areas, predominantly the Charleston Park subdivision. Our proposed main entrance from West Lake Street lines up directly across from the entrance to Charleston Park. The entrance location is ideal from a public safety, traffic management, aesthetic, and natural resource conversation perspective. The site is designed to minimize impact to residents of Charleston Park. Our proposed entrance opens to a captivating view of the club house, landscaping, and amenities. Units within the site are set back substantially from West Lake Street, across a wetland and a wooded buffer.

<a id="p49"></a>
*[Page 49 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=49)* (read by OCR; check the original)

To the west is Green Oak Township. The Dixboro Road frontage is largely existing developed (currently Westlake Place Apartments) and our proposal enhances the existing entrance with a boulevard entrance to the multi-family phase, and enhanced retention pond with the intention of maintaining surface water. The proposed industrial building is perpendicular to Dixboro Road, and extra attention is given to the road design and curb appeal.

Explain how the requested rezoning is consistent with the goals, policies, and Future Land Use Map of the City of South Lyon Master Plan, including any sub-area or corridor studies. If conditions have changed since the Plan was adopted, the consistency with recent development trends in the area:

The proposed rezoning fills a long-standing desire for this Property to be industrially developed. This property has been zoned, partially developed, and vacant for almost 25 years. Our proposal advances that intention in part, and importantly, to the degree of absorption that is appropriate for the City of South Lyon.

Equally important is the City of South Lyon's need for affordable, non-owner occupied, Missing Middle housing for professionals and upwardly mobile singles. This demand is partially met in the recently developed existing Westlake Place Apartments, which are fully absorbed and stabilized. Our expansion comes closer to filling that need and providing the citizens of South Lyon with more upscale affordable housing options with a rich amenity mix tailored to modern lifestyle desires.

The uses must have a beneficial effect, in terms of public health, safety, welfare, or convenience, on present and future potential surrounding land uses. The uses proposed must not adversely affect the public utility and circulation system, surrounding properties, or the environment. The public benefit shall be one which could not be achieved under the regulations of the underlying district alone or that of any other zoning district.

Beneficial effects to the public and to surrounding land uses of our proposal include:

Paving Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southern property line, approximately 1088' from the corner has a clear and obvious effect on public health, safety welfare and convenience to our residents and to the Industrial users and their customers.

Paving a connection to the existing sidewalk along Lake Street, dramatically improving non-motorized accessibility to the City downtown for our residents and neighborhoods.

Paving a connection between development and the Huron Valley Rail Trail (entire length of Dixboro Road from West Lake Street to trail head), dramatically improving accessibility to residents and neighborhoods. (This is already a requirement under the current Westlake Place development)

Public safety is enhanced by increasing / creating non-motorized circulation without forcing road crossings.

Removal of existing West Lake Street entrance to Westlake Place Apartments and creating new entrance creates a much safer traffic flow environment and is aesthetically superior for a host of reasons.

Placement of 'Welcome to South Lyon' sign at the corner of West Lake Street and Dixboro Road will enhance 10 Mile corridor from the west.

We hope you share our view that this application a fantastic opportunity to put a long-dormant property to use, provide the city with a healthy balance of industrial/business flex space and multi-family residents and enhance the Lake Street / Dixboro entrance to the City.

<a id="p54"></a>
*[Page 54 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=54)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Executive Summary

Introduction – This interim and draft Executive Summary supports a Residential Target Market Analysis prepared for Elevate Land Holdings, LLC (hereinafter, "Elevate") in the Summer of 2023. The deliverables have included four documents as follows: 1) Residential Target Market Analysis; 2) Real Estate Analysis; 3) Demographic Analysis; and 4) Economic Analysis.

This narrative focuses primarily on the Residential Target Market Analysis, hereinafter the "housing study". Excerpts from the complete study have been assembled into a relatively short Handout for distribution to the City of South Lyon's Planning Commission. One of the exhibits (a scatter plot) in the Handout also have been excerpted from the Real Estate Analysis. Electronic copies of all four documents can be made available to the City upon request, recognizing that additional narrative reports are still underway.

Project Description – The Westlake PUD site includes roughly 40 acres located at the southeast quadrant of 10 Mile Road (W Lake Street) and Dixboro Road in the City of South Lyon, Michigan. Nearby uses include an industrial mill to the southeast and detached houses to the north and west (generally on cul-de-sacs). The northern entrance into the project is ideally located just 0.7 miles west of South Lyon's downtown.

If approved as envisioned, the total Westlake PUD project eventually would include three phases of apartment buildings, with Phase One already completed (72 units), and Phase Two (144 units) and Phase Three (180 units) proposed. The latter two phases would collectively add about 324 new apartment units, ideally over the course of three years. A clubhouse, pool, and spacious dog park also are proposed as amenities to help intercept migrating households. Detached garages might be available for some of the units, and extra storage space also is being explored.

LandUseUSA toured the South Lyon market, visited the downtown, and conducted a site visit in mid-2023. Based on the results of the market analysis (with additional details on the following pages) and the site assessment, we are in support of Elevate's Westlake PUD residential and industrial mixed-use project and recommend that the developer proceed with confidence that the rents can meet and perhaps even exceed expectations. Rent will easily be 10% higher than Phase One, and up to 20% higher than city-wide averages.

We also recommend that Elevate target single professionals of all ages, with incomes that can tolerate minimum rents of $1,350 per month for studio apartments up to $2,400 per month for corner units with two bedrooms. Finally, we recommend that the industrial component of the project be no larger than 65,000 square feet and no smaller than 65,000 square feet. In other words, the size of that proposed industrial space is just right.

<a id="p55"></a>
*[Page 55 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=55)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Market Potential

City-Wide Market Potential – Based on the results of the housing study, about 285 new renter households are migrating into the City of South Lyon each year, unadjusted for out-migration. If new for-lease units can be built in the city, then it will not only intercept these new renters, but also help intercept out-migration, resulting in net population growth.

In addition to in-migration, about 110 existing renter households are migrating within South Lyon each year by moving from one address to another within the same city. Lastly, about 55 households are bypassing the city each year but could potentially be intercepted if new for-lease choices become available. Collectively, these three components represent a maximum, not-to-exceed market potential for 450 new for-lease units throughout the city each year. See below for a table summary.

<div>Summary of the Annual Market Potential For-Lease New-Build Units The City of South Lyon, Michigan</div>

<table><tr><td>Summary</td><td>Type of</td><td>Total</td><td>Apt</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Potential</td><td>Movership</td><td>Units</td><td>Units</td><td>Strategy</td></tr><tr><td>Capture</td><td>In-Migration</td><td>285</td><td>100</td><td>Cautious</td></tr><tr><td>Retain</td><td>Internal Migration</td><td>110</td><td>35</td><td>Bold</td></tr><tr><td>Intercept</td><td>Bypassing Migration</td><td>55</td><td>20</td><td>Aggressive</td></tr><tr><td>Maximum</td><td>Aggregate Migration</td><td>450</td><td>155</td><td></td></tr></table>

Of the 450 renter households migrating into, within, and bypassing the city each year, only 155 of them will be inclined to lease an apartment or urban loft. The balance will either seek townhouses with private entrances and small porches (45 households); and detached units like subdivided houses, cottages, and/or accessory dwellings (250 households).

Similarly, of the 450 renter households migrating into, within, and bypassing the city each year, only one-third will tolerate rents that are in the same threshold as Westlake PUD's current units (about $1,300 and above). This generally aligns with the assessment of formats similarly points to a limit of about 155 units annually in Phase Two and Phase Three of the Westlake PUD.

<a id="p56"></a>
*[Page 56 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=56)* (read by OCR; check the original)

It also is important to note that if new competing apartment complexes are developed at the same time as the Westlake PUD, then they would need to share the maximum annual market potential for 155 units. To stay on the safe side, Westlake PUD probably should plan on building no more than 100 units in any given year, over the course of three years until build-out is achieved.

In terms of price, only one-third of the migrating renter households will tolerate rents of $1,300 per month or more. There also is a segment of the market that would tolerate higher rents up to $1,700 per month, and a few households will even tolerate prices up to $2,300. Households that can tolerate rents above $2,300 will either look for houses (or luxury townhouses) to lease; or they could be converted into home buyers.

## Real Estate Analysis

This housing study includes a detailed Real Estate Analysis that compares the asking rents, square feet, and rent per square foot among existing housing choices throughout the City of South Lyon, the four surrounding townships (Green Oak, Lyon, Salem, and Northfield townships, in clockwise order), and some nearby places like Brighton and Wixom. Based on the results, there is an upside potential for the Westlake PUD to increase monthly contract rents on new-build units in Phases Two and Three (compared to existing units in Phase One) by about +$125 per square foot for the smallest units, and +$150 per square foot for the largest units.

The smallest one-bedroom apartments with 600 square feet could have rents of about $1,500 per month; one-bedroom units with 700 square feet could have rents of $1,575 per month; small two-bedroom units with 800 square feet could have rents of $1,680 per month; and larger two-bedroom units with 1,000 square feet could have rents approaching $2,000 per month.

Although smaller units tend to have lower monthly rents, they have higher rents per square foot. In other words, smaller units are more efficient, whereas the rents per square foot progressively erode for larger units. Smaller units also are more likely to appeal to singles with one wage-earner, and especially the “Bohemian Groove”, “Full Steam Ahead”, “Striving Single” target markets. Units with lower rents also tend to have the highest absorption rates.

However, we also recognize that the City of South Lyon is high-quality market and a destination for families, such as the "Family Troopers". Therefore, we have recommended that Elevate strive for a good balance between one-bedroom, two-bedroom, and three-bedroom units. We also have recommended that the new units be provided with larger balconies than Phase One.

<a id="p57"></a>
*[Page 57 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=57)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Industrial Component

Industrial Component – The proposed Westlake PUD project also includes four light industrial buildings with a collective 63,000 to 65,000 square feet located along the southern edge of the site. Based on the Elevate's preliminary concepts, potential light industrial tenants include research & development, shipping & receiving, machine shops, professional contractors, and other small businesses that are more inclined to lease than own space. Note: The Beck Business Center (located at the northwest quadrant of I-96 and Beck Road in nearby Wixom) has been discussed as possible analog.

Elevate has asked LandUseUSA to comment on the optimal size and mix of uses in the industrial component of the Westlake PUD. Based on our market assessment and field observations, we believe that the project could attract some small level of interest from non-industrial small businesses like a microbrewery, fitness center, health care practitioners, veterinarian, marketing agencies, insurance agencies, and attorney offices. However, many of these uses might also be interested in locating at the fringes of Downtown South Lyon or along the Pontiac Trail corridor.

Based on our assessment of the site's locational attributes, and especially visibility to vehicular traffic, we are concerned that a smaller project would struggle to survive without the necessary critical mass. On the flip side, a larger project could run the risk of siphoning small businesses away from the downtown.

Lastly, a larger project might struggle to attract enough quality tenants to fill the space, resulting in high vacancy, which in turn could undermines rents and profitability. Note: Vacancy rates will be less of a concern for other projects that are located Pontiac Trail and that have better exposure to vehicular traffic.

For the reasons described above, LandUseUSA recommends that Elevate proceed with the Industrial component of the project, but with caution. Good occupancy rates and rents can be optimized with a flexible design that appeals to a variety of businesses and that also is adaptable over time and to each tenant's unique needs. In short, 65,000 square feet of space feels about right – anything less would be too small, and anything more would be too large.

Prepared August 6, 2023

Sharon Woods, President

Sharon M. Woods

LandUseUSA | Urban Strategies

## 71 Lifestyle Clusters - General Approach Experian Decision Analytics; 2020 - 2023

More than 300 data points have been used to build Mosaic USA. These have been selected as inputs to the classification on the basis of their coverage, quality, consistency and sustainability.

The data variables enable accurate identification and differentiation between a wide range of consumer characteristics and attributes. (See the list below.)

The data variables are updated quarterly to ensure continued accuracy in assignments of the Mosaic codes.

In general, they meet the following criteria:

- Allow the identification and description of consumer segments that are not necessarily distinguished solely by the use of census data.

- Ensure accuracy of Mosaic code by either household or neighborhood.

• Are updated regularly to ensure that changes are monitored.

- Improve differentiation and allow for the identification of a wide range of consumer behaviors.

Traffic Impact Study</td></tr></table>

## 1 INTRODUCTION

This memorandum presents the results of the Traffic Impact Study (TIS) for the proposed West Lake Planned Unit Development (PUD) in the City of South Lyon, Michigan. The project site is generally located in the southeast quadrant of the 10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road intersection, as shown on the attached Figure 1.

The purpose of this study is to provide a TIS and Parking Study for the West Lake PUD, as currently proposed. The existing West Lake PUD Phase 1 has been completed and Phases 2, 3, and 4 are currently proposed. Site access for Phase 1 was previously constructed and provided via one (1) site driveway on Dixboro Road and one (1) site driveway on 10-Mile Road. As part of this project, the existing site access on 10-Mile Road will be closed and a new shared driveway will be constructed on 10-Mile Road, aligned directly opposite of the existing Singh Boulevard. Additional site access is proposed via two (2) new driveways on Dixboro Road. The study roadways are under the jurisdiction of the Road Commission for Oakland County (RCOC). The City of South Lyon has required a TIS for this project as part of the PUD approval process.

The scope of the study was developed based on Fleis & VandenBrink's (F&V) understanding of the development program, accepted traffic engineering practice, the requirements of the City of South Lyon and RCOC, and professional experience. The study intersection analyses were completed using Synchro/SimTraffic (Version 11) traffic analysis software. Sources of data for this study include F&V subconsultant Quality Counts (QC), information published by the Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE), the City of South Lyon, the Southeast Michigan Council of Governments (SEMCOG), and RCOC.

## 2 BACKGROUND

### 2.1 EXISTING ROAD NETWORK

The lane uses and traffic control at the study intersections are shown on the attached Figure 2 and the study roadways are further described below. For the purposes of this study, site driveways and residential streets were assumed to have an operating speed of 25 miles per hour (mph) unless otherwise noted.

10-Mile Road (W. Lake Street) generally runs in the east and west directions, adjacent to the north side of the project site. The study section of 10-Mile Road is under the jurisdiction of RCOC, is classified as a Minor Arterial, has an Average Annual Daily Traffic (AADT) volume of approximately 9,000 (SEMCOG 2018) vehicles per day (vpd), and provides a typical two-lane cross-section, with one (1) lane of travel in each direction. 10-Mile Road has posted speed limits of 35-mph and 45-mph, to the east and west of Dixboro Road, respectively.

27725 Stansbury Boulevard, Suite 195

Farmington Hills, MI 48334

<a id="p96"></a>
*[Page 96 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=96)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Dixboro Road runs in the north and south directions, adjacent to the west side of the project site. Dixboro Road is under the jurisdiction of RCOC, is classified as a Major Collector, has a prima facie speed limit of 55-mph, and has an AADT volume of approximately 500 vpd (SEMCOG 2018). The study section of Dixboro Road is unpaved and provides a typical two-lane cross section, with one (1) lane of travel in each direction.

Edenderry Drive generally runs in the east and west directions, aligned directly opposite the proposed industrial driveway. Edenderry Drive is classified as a Local Road, is under the jurisdiction of RCOC, and provides a typical two-lane cross-section, with one (1) lane of travel in each direction. The roadway does not provide a posted speed limit; therefore, a prima facie residential speed limit of 25-mph was assumed.

### 2.2 EXISTING TRAFFIC VOLUMES

F&V subconsultant QC collected existing Turning Movement Count (TMC) data on March 21 $ ^{st} $, 2023, during the AM (7:00 AM to 9:00 AM) and PM (4:00 PM to 6:00 PM) peak periods, at the following study intersections:

10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road

Dixboro Road & Edenderry Road

During collection of the turning movement counts, Peak Hour Factors (PHFs) and commercial truck percentages were recorded and used in the traffic analysis. The peak hour traffic volumes for each intersection were utilized for this study; the volumes were balanced upward through the study network and balanced through the proposed site driveways. Therefore, the raw traffic volumes shown in the data collection may not match the traffic volumes used in the analysis and on the attached traffic volume figures.

The weekday AM and PM peak hours for the adjacent roadway network were observed to generally occur between 7:00 AM to 8:00 AM and 4:30 PM to 5:30 PM, respectively. F&V collected an inventory of existing lane use and traffic controls, as shown on the attached Figure 2.

## 3 EXISTING CONDITIONS (2023)

Existing peak hour vehicle delays and Levels of Service (LOS) were calculated at the study intersections using Synchro/SimTraffic (Version 11) traffic analysis software. The study analyses were based on the existing lane use and traffic control shown on the attached Figure 2, the existing peak hour traffic volumes shown on the attached Figure 3, and the methodologies presented in the Highway Capacity Manual, 6th Edition (HCM6).

Descriptions of LOS "A" through "F" as defined in the HCM6, are attached. Typically, LOS D is considered acceptable, with LOS A representing minimal delay, and LOS F indicating failing conditions. Additionally, SimTraffic network simulations were reviewed to evaluate network operations and vehicle queues. The existing conditions results are attached and summarized in Table 1.

<div>Table 1: Existing Intersection Operations</div>

<table><tr><td rowspan="3" colspan="2">Intersection</td><td rowspan="3" colspan="2">Control Approach</td><td colspan="4">Existing Conditions</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2">AM Peak</td><td colspan="2">PM Peak</td></tr><tr><td>Delay (s/veh)</td><td>LOS</td><td>Delay (s/veh)</td><td>LOS</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="3">1</td><td rowspan="3">10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road</td><td rowspan="3">Stop (Minor)</td><td>EB</td><td colspan="4">Free</td></tr><tr><td>WBL</td><td>9.3</td><td>A</td><td>8.2</td><td>A</td></tr><tr><td>NB</td><td>17.2</td><td>C</td><td>15.2</td><td>C</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="4">2</td><td rowspan="4">Dixboro Road & Edenderry Drive / Industrial Site Drive</td><td rowspan="4">Stop (Minor)</td><td>EB</td><td>9.5</td><td>A</td><td>9.4</td><td>A</td></tr><tr><td>WB</td><td>0.0 $ ^{{*}} $</td><td>A $ ^{{*}} $</td><td>0.0 $ ^{{*}} $</td><td>A $ ^{{*}} $</td></tr><tr><td>NBL</td><td>7.5</td><td>A</td><td>7.3</td><td>A</td></tr><tr><td>SBL</td><td>0.0</td><td>A</td><td>0.0</td><td>A</td></tr></table>

 $ ^{*} $Indicates no vehicle volume present

The result of the existing conditions analysis indicates that all approaches and movements at the study intersections are currently operating acceptably, at LOS D or better, during both the AM and PM peak periods.

A review of SimTraffic network simulations also indicates acceptable operations throughout the study roadway network during both peak periods.

<a id="p97"></a>
*[Page 97 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=97)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## 4 BACKGROUND (2027) CONDITIONS

Population and economic growth profile data for the City of South Lyon was obtained from the Southeast Michigan Council of Governments (SEMCOG) database to calculate a background growth rate to project the 2023 peak hour traffic volumes to the site buildout year of 2027. Population and employment projections from 2020 to 2050 were reviewed and showed an average annual growth of 0.32% and 0.45%, respectively. Therefore, a conservative annual background growth rate of 0.5% per year was applied to the existing peak hour traffic volumes to forecast the background 2027 peak hour traffic volumes, without the proposed development. Additionally, it is important to account for developments within the study network, which will be constructed prior to the site buildout year of 2027, the following development was identified within the study network:

## Charleston Park: 195 unit Single-Family Detached

The number of weekday peak hour (AM and PM) and daily vehicle trips that would be generated by the Charleston Park development were forecast based on data published by ITE in the Trip Generation Manual, $ 11^{th} $ Edition. The background development trip generation projections are summarized in Table 2.

In order to provide a conservative analysis of the study roadway network, the development was assumed to be unoccupied; the full trip generation for the neighborhood was then projected and distributed onto the study roadway network. Therefore, the existing peak hour traffic volumes were combined with the background development site-generated trips, after applying the calculated background growth rate of 0.5%, in order to forecast the background (2027) traffic volumes without the proposed development.

## 7 FUTURE CONDITIONS

The future peak hour vehicle delays and LOS, with the addition of the proposed development, was based on the proposed lane use and traffic control shown on the attached Figure 2, the proposed site access plan, the future peak hour traffic volumes shown on the attached Figure 6, and the methodologies presented in the HCM6. The results of the future conditions analysis are attached and summarized in Table 6.

The results of the future conditions analysis indicate that all study intersection approaches and movements will continue to operate acceptably, at LOS D or better during both peak periods, in a manner similar to the background conditions analysis, with the exception of the following:

## 10 -Mile Road & Dixboro Road

•  $ \underline{\text{During the AM peak hour}} $: The northbound approach is expected to operate at LOS E.

Although the intersection LOS analysis indicates poor operations for the northbound approach during the AM peak period, a review of SimTraffic microsimulations indicates acceptable operations. SimTraffic network

<a id="p99"></a>
*[Page 99 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=99)* (read by OCR; check the original)

simulations indicate that vehicles on the northbound approach were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing. The 95th percentile vehicle queue length reported for the northbound approach during the AM peak period was approximately 82-feet (3-4 vehicles), which is not significant.

## 10 -Mile Road & Site Drive #1 / Singh Boulevard

- During the PM peak hour: The northbound and southbound left-turn movements are expected to operate at LOS E.

Although the intersection LOS analysis indicates poor operations associated with the minor street left-turn movements during the PM peak period, a review of SimTraffic microsimulations indicates acceptable operations. SimTraffic network simulations indicate that vehicles on the northbound and southbound left-turn movements were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing. The 95th percentile vehicle queue length reported for the northbound and southbound left-turn movement was approximately 53-feet (2-3 vehicles) or less, which is not significant. Additionally, aligning the proposed Site Drive #1 directly opposite Singh Boulevard follows best access management practices.

## 8 PARKING ANALYSIS

The proposed development plan for the West Lake PUD site was evaluated to determine the recommended number of parking spaces for the residential uses of the site. The Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE) and the South Lyon parking ordinances were both used for this analysis.

The Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE) Parking Generation, 5th Edition was used to determine the parking demand for this site. The ITE Parking Generation is an informational guide used by engineers and planners for the purposes of determining the parking demand associated with various land uses. The parking generation data included in Parking Generation are provided by various state and local government agencies.

<a id="p100"></a>
*[Page 100 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=100)* (read by OCR; check the original)

consulting firms, individual transportation professionals, universities, developers, associations, local sections, districts, and student chapters of ITE located throughout the U.S. The data is examined by ITE for validity and reasonableness before being entered into the comprehensive database. Therefore, the data presented by ITE in the Parking Generation provides a comprehensive average of parking demand for the various land uses throughout the country, and is a recommended resource for the calculation parking demand.

The  $ 85^{th} $ percentile peak parking demand rates from ITE Parking Generation,  $ 5^{th} $ Edition were used in this analysis. An 85% percentile means that of 85% of similar sites survived for the calculation had peak parking demands at or below this rate. This provides a conservative evaluation when calculating the projected parking. The results of this analysis are summarized in Table 6.

<div>Table 6: ITE Parking Generation Parking Demand Summary</div>

<table><tr><td rowspan="3">Land Use</td><td rowspan="3">ITE Land Use Code</td><td rowspan="3">Building</td><td rowspan="3">Size</td><td rowspan="3">Independent Variable</td><td colspan="4">ITE Parking Generation 5th Edition</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="2">Peak Period Parking 85th% Demand Rates Weekday</td><td rowspan="2">Peak Parking Demand Rates Weekend</td><td colspan="2">Peak Parking Demand (veh)</td></tr><tr><td>Weekday</td><td>Weekend</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="5">Multi-Family Housing Mid-Rise</td><td rowspan="5">221</td><td>Phase 1</td><td>123</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>107</td><td>95</td></tr><tr><td>Phase 2</td><td>153</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>133</td><td>118</td></tr><tr><td>Phase 3</td><td>153</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>133</td><td>118</td></tr><tr><td>Phase 4</td><td>153</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>133</td><td>118</td></tr><tr><td>Total</td><td>582</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>506</td><td>449</td></tr></table>

The ITE Trip Generation 11th Edition LUC 220 describes Multi-Family (Low-Rise) as between 1-3 levels of residential living. The ITE Parking Generation 5th is based upon LUC 221 from the ITE Trip Generation 10th Edition that includes buildings with 3-10 levels of residence.

The projected parking demand calculated was compared to the proposed parking supply for this site to determine if there is adequate parking to accommodate the proposed operations. The highest daily parking demands for this development are expected to occur on the weekdays. The results of the analysis are summarized in Table 7 and show that the proposed parking supply on site will accommodate the projected parking demand, with a peak occupancy of less than 90% utilization.

The peak parking demand for this site was also compared to the City of South Lyon Ordinance requirements which requires 1.5 parking spaces per each dwelling unit. The results of this analysis are summarized in Table 7 and show the City parking supply requirements for this site have a projected surplus parking spaces and would have the site significantly overparked based on the ordinance requirements.

## 9 KEY FINDINGS

The key findings of this TIS are as follows:

1.  $ \underline{\text{Existing Conditions}} $: All approaches and movements at the study intersections are currently operating acceptably, at LOS D or better during both the AM and PM peak hours.

### 2. Background Growth

- A background growth rate of 0.5% was calculated to project traffic volumes to the buildout year of 2027.

- The Charleston Park development (195 single family units) was included as background traffic volumes.

The Charleston Park development (195 single family units) was included as background traffic volumes.

<a id="p101"></a>
*[Page 101 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/3-11-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202403071604390#page=101)* (read by OCR; check the original)

3. Background Conditions: All approaches and movements at the study intersections are expected to continue operating acceptably, at LOS D or better during both peak periods, in a manner similar to the existing conditions analysis, with minor increases in delay.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Future Conditions}} $ All approaches and movements at the study intersections are expected to continue operating acceptably, at LOS D or better during both peak periods, in a manner similar to the background conditions analysis, with the exception of the following:

- 10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road: The northbound Dixboro Road approach is expected to operate at LOS E during the AM peak hour. Review of Sim Traffic shows a 95th percentile queue length of approximately 82-feet (3-4 vehicles). Vehicles were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing.

- 10-Mile Road & Singh Boulevard / Site Drive #1: The northbound left-turn and southbound left-turn movements are expected to operate at LOS E during the PM peak hour. A review of SimTraffic network simulations shows a 95th percentile queue length of approximately 53-feet (2-3 vehicles). Left-turning vehicles were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing.

### 5. $ \underline{\text{Parking Analysis}} $

- The results of the parking show that the proposed residential parking supply will accommodate the projected parking demand, with a peak occupancy of less than 90% utilization.

The peak parking demand for this site was also compared to the City of South Lyon Ordinance requirements and show the City parking supply requirements for this site have a projected surplus parking spaces and would have the site significantly overparked based on the ordinance requirements.

## 10 CONCLUSIONS

The conclusions of this TIS are as follows:

- The existing study roadway network can adequately accommodate the projected site-generated traffic volumes. No mitigation measures are recommended.

Any questions related to this memorandum, study, analysis, and results should be addressed to Fleis & VandenBrink.
